# Parallels index: the Ugaritic corpus

The key findings (written by the coordinator when the run ends), then the table: every **הקבלה** row of the annotated
files, filed by its first Bible verse (generated by `build_parallels_index.py`; do not edit the table by hand).

## Key findings

Round 4 run 1, 2026-10-23 (first draft: the whole Baal Cycle KTU 1.1–1.6 and eight short texts, 1,749 lines; the
fifth-day dossier in `3-תיק-היום-החמישי/`). Every item is in the table below with its line and tag.

**For the fifth day (Gen 1:20–23)**
1. **The sea monsters are enemies, never creatures.** *tnn* is the defeated or muzzled foe: Anat's boast "I muzzled
   the *tnn*" (1.3 III:40, asseverative *l*, not a question: Isa 51:9's question form is a [divergence]); Šapšu rules
   over "in the sea – Arš and the *tnn*" (1.6 VI:51 = Isa 27:1 `הַתַּנִּין אֲשֶׁר בַּיָּם`); 1.82:1 beside *mḫṣ* (Job
   26:12; uncertain syntax). Gen 1:21 `וַיִּבְרָא … אֶת הַתַּנִּינִם הַגְּדֹלִים` makes the same word a created kind: the
   verb `בָּרָא` against Ugarit's *mḫṣ* / *šbm* is the finding. **1.83:8 has *tan*, not *tnn*** (CUC = KTU³): the "tnn
   bound and muzzled" there is an emendation and is cited only as such.
2. **Leviathan's adjectives belong to a *bṯn*.** *ltn bṯn brḥ … bṯn ʿqltn* (1.5 I:1–2; 1.3 III:41–42) has the very
   adjectives of Isa 27:1 (`נָחָשׁ בָּרִחַ … נָחָשׁ עֲקַלָּתוֹן`); Isaiah kept the name and the adjectives and changed
   the noun to `נָחָשׁ` (*bṯn* = `פֶּתֶן` [cognate], Ps 58:5, 91:13). Ugarit's mythic serpent is never *nḥš*: *nḥš* is
   the snake of the charms (1.100, 1.107).
3. **Cosmic vocabulary that Gen 1–2 shares:** *thw* `תֹּהוּ` (1.5 I:15, Mot's appetite) [cognate]; *thmtm* `שְׁתֵּי
   הַתְּהוֹמוֹת` at El's dwelling (1.4 IV:22; Gen 49:25, Deut 33:13 `תְּהוֹם רֹבֶצֶת תָּחַת`); *rḫp* of Anat "hovering"
   (1.108:8; Gen 1:2, Deut 32:11) [cognate]; the seven-day count of the fire in Baal's palace (1.4 VI:24–32 `בַּיּוֹם
   הַשְּׁבִיעִי`; Gen 2:2) [divergence: the seventh day ends a work, not a rest].

**Further key parallels**
4. **Kingship won at the sea:** 1.2 IV:10 "you will take your eternal kingship" after Yam's defeat ↔ Ex 15:18, Ps
   145:13, 29:10, 93:1–4; the step-by-step line of 1.2 IV:9 ↔ Ps 92:10; the clubs "named" by Kothar ↔ the divine
   naming; Habakkuk 3:8 and Nahum 1:4 (`גּוֹעֵר בַּיָּם`) against a goddess who *rebukes* Baal (*gʿr*).
5. **Anat destroys Mot with Moses' verbs** (1.6 II:31–35): *šrp* `שָׂרַף`, *ṭḥn* `טָחַן`, *ḏry* `זָרָה` – the three
   verbs of the golden calf (Ex 32:20) [equivalent].
6. **The Rephaim and Og's towns:** 1.6 VI:46 `הָרְפָאִים`; 1.108:2–3 Rapiʾu "who dwells in Ashtaroth, who rules in
   Edrei" ↔ Deut 1:4, Josh 12:4, Deut 3:11 (Og, the last of the Rephaim).
7. **Small words with large echoes:** 1.24:7 `הֵן, הָעַלְמָה תֵּלֵד בֵּן` ↔ Isa 7:14; *mhr* `מֹהַר` (1.24; Gen 34:12,
   Ex 22:16) and *ṯlḫ* `שִׁלּוּחִים` (1 Kgs 9:16); *mrzḥ* `מַרְזֵחַ` (1.114, 1.1 IV; Amos 6:7, Jer 16:5); *ʿz* // *ḏmr*
   (1.108; Ex 15:2 `עָזִּי וְזִמְרָת`); "one lip to earth, one to heaven" (1.5 II:2, 1.23) and *lšnm tlhk* (1.83:5) ↔
   Ps 73:9; *ʿzm kmt* ↔ Song 8:6 `עַזָּה כַמָּוֶת` (1.6 VI:17); Mot's mourning rites (1.5 VI – 1.6 I) ↔ Gen 37:35, Mic
   1:10, Deut 14:1, Jer 48:37.
8. **A famous parallel that is not on the tablet:** 1.23:14 is *ṭb gd b ḥlb* "good coriander in milk" – no kid and no
   mother (Ratner & Zuckerman 1986): Ex 23:19, 34:26, Deut 14:21 are tagged [absent], with the history in the apparatus.

**Readings corrected against the machine parse** (each a note in its line): *ʿn* "furrow" `מַעֲנָה` (Ps 129:3), not
"Anat" (1.6 IV:1–2, 12–13); *mhr* "bride-price", not "warrior" (1.24); *rḫp*, not *rḥp* (1.108:8); *l* asseverative in
1.3 III:38–46.

**The ancient mind** (the הערות): a god's death and the mourning of El and Anat, gashing and dust; El drunk at his
*marzeaḥ*, the dog under the table and the hangover cure (1.114); a mare who calls god after god, city after city,
until Horon answers (1.100); a marriage negotiated with a bride-price and refused rivals (1.24); a palace that a god must
beg permission to build, with a window that the rains need.
